# InboundScope refactor — eliminate bot_id leaks across the inbound→outbound path

## Why this exists

The Veas codebase is being extended from a single-bot mediator (Véas) to a multi-bot platform. The second bot, Tante Rosi (German pregnancy coach, solo-shape, Discord-only), keeps misrouting: messages sent to Rosi get processed and replied by Véas, with the wrong persona, language, and topic.

We've now patched the *same shape of bug* three times in rapid succession:

1. `app/services/inbound.py::_resolve_scope` had `bot_id: str = "mediator"` as a silent default → inbound rows tagged with the wrong bot. (Fixed in commit `5a38d7f`.)
2. `app/main.py` constructed a single `app.state.coalescer` with `bot_id="mediator"` captured in closures used as the dispatch callback. The burst data structure already carried the correct per-event bot_id, but the closure ignored it. (Fixed in commit `f29bc03` by moving to `app.state.coalescers: dict[str, BurstCoalescer]` and resolving topic via `get_bot_spec(bot_id) + primary_topic_id_for(pool, spec)`.)
3. `app/services/recovery.py`, `app/services/vision.py`, `app/services/transcription.py` still hardcode `bot_id='mediator'` in their own outbound calls. (Not yet fixed.)

After both patches (1) and (2) were on `main`, the user reports the wrong bot is **still responding**. So there is at least one more leak we haven't characterised yet, plus the three known hardcodes above.

**This is no longer a patching problem — it's a structural one.** Each fix surfaces another instance of the same shape because `bot_id` was bolted onto a codebase that was written assuming a single mediator. It rides through the system as a loose parameter: sometimes a function arg, sometimes a closure capture, sometimes a DB column, sometimes a default. The boundary between "this code knows its bot" and "this code looks it up" is fuzzy and inconsistent.

## What we want

Introduce a single explicit **`InboundScope`** dataclass (frozen) that:

- Is constructed **once** at each gateway boundary (Discord `_handle_message`, Discord `_handle_reaction_add`, Discord `catch_up_recent_messages`, WhatsApp router).
- Carries **all per-event identity**: `bot_id`, `transport`, `user_id` (sender), `topic_id`, `channel_id`, `binding_id`, `dyad_id` (None for solo-shape bots like Rosi).
- Threads explicitly through every downstream code path: `process_inbound` → coalescer → burst dispatch → agentic turn → LLM tools → outbound (text, voice, image) → DB writes.
- **Replaces** the existing loose `bot_id` kwarg pattern. No function below the gateway accepts `bot_id` as an optional kwarg with a `"mediator"` default. Forgetting to pass scope becomes a type error.

The existing `ResolvedScope` NamedTuple in `app/services/inbound.py` (around line 161) is the seed of this — it already has `(bot_id, topic_id, channel_id, binding_id, dyad_id)`. **Promote it to a frozen dataclass, add `transport` and `user_id`, rename to `InboundScope`, and make it the universal carrier.**

There is also an existing `app/services/turn_context.py` — investigate during prep whether it already plays a related role and whether it should be merged with / replaced by `InboundScope`, or whether they have distinct responsibilities (per-event identity vs per-turn execution context).

## Done looks like

1. **Defined**: `InboundScope` dataclass exists in one canonical location (probably `app/services/inbound.py` or a new `app/services/scope.py`). Frozen, fully typed, no defaults.
2. **Threaded**: every function from gateway → outbound takes scope (or scope-derived values) explicitly. Audited list of touched files includes at minimum:
   - `app/services/discord.py` (gateway handlers: `_handle_message`, `_handle_reaction_add`, `catch_up_recent_messages`)
   - `app/routers/whatsapp.py` (WA webhook → constructs `InboundScope(bot_id="mediator", transport="whatsapp", ...)` explicitly — single-WA-bot is fine for now)
   - `app/services/inbound.py` (`process_inbound`, `_resolve_scope`)
   - `app/services/debouncer.py` (coalescer: `add(scope, ...)`, burst carries scope, on_complete callback uses `burst.scope` not closure)
   - `app/services/agentic.py` and/or `app/services/turn_context.py` (agentic turn takes scope)
   - `app/services/tools/*` (LLM tools: `send_text`, distillation tools, etc. — take scope or scope-derived bot_id/topic_id, no defaults)
   - `app/services/messaging.py` (outbound send: takes scope; the OOB delivery-time guardrail also needs to be bot-aware — see open question below)
   - `app/services/transcription.py` (voice path: takes scope; remove `bot_id='mediator'` hardcode at lines ~77, 94, 118)
   - `app/services/vision.py` (image path: takes scope; remove `bot_id='mediator'` hardcode at lines ~198, 218, 225)
   - `app/services/recovery.py` (startup replay: reconstructs scope from DB row via a helper `scope_from_message_row(row)`; remove `bot_id='mediator'` hardcode at line ~114)
   - `app/services/hot_context.py` and `app/services/hot_context_solo.py` (audit: confirm they take topic_id/scope cleanly; solo variant must work with `dyad_id=None`)
   - `app/services/prompts.py` and `app/services/prompts_solo.py` (audit: confirm renderer pick is by scope.dyad_id is None or by bot spec; no hidden "always mediator" assumption)
   - `app/services/oob_check.py` (audit: the delivery-time OOB guardrail must be scoped per bot, not global to recipient)
3. **Defaults removed**: zero occurrences of `bot_id: str = "mediator"` or `bot_id="mediator"` in `app/services/**` and `app/routers/**` after the refactor. Grep verification is part of the success criteria.
4. **Closure leaks removed**: the coalescer's `on_burst_complete` callback reads `burst.scope` (or `burst.bot_id`) — does **not** capture bot_id from the surrounding closure. The per-bot dict introduced in `f29bc03` may stay or may collapse to one shared coalescer; design call during prep/plan.
5. **Tests updated**: existing test suite passes (`pytest` from repo root). Tests that construct fake calls into `process_inbound`, `send_text`, etc. pass a proper `InboundScope` fixture rather than ad-hoc kwargs.
6. **Behavior verified**: after the refactor, a DM to Rosi (Discord user ID `1401572203954311229`) gets a German reply from Rosi's persona with the pregnancy topic; a DM to Véas gets an English reply from Véas's persona with the relationship topic. Both bots online simultaneously, no cross-leaks. (Manual verification by user post-deploy; the plan should describe how to test it.)

## Constraints / invariants to preserve

- **Don't break Véas.** Véas is in production and being used. The refactor must preserve current dyadic-mediator behavior end-to-end.
- **Don't break WhatsApp.** WA is currently mediator-only; the WA router must continue to work with `InboundScope(bot_id="mediator", transport="whatsapp", ...)`.
- **Don't change DB schemas** in this refactor. The `bot_id` column already exists on messages, channels, etc. The refactor is code-only.
- **Don't change the LLM prompts** for either bot (other than threading the right one for the right turn). Persona templates already exist in `app/services/prompts.py` and `app/services/prompts_solo.py`.
- **Don't break the per-bot Discord gateway architecture** introduced earlier. Each bot has its own `DiscordClient`, `DiscordGatewayBot`, and `DiscordPacer`. These are correctly per-bot already — the leak is downstream of the gateway, not at the gateway.
- **Don't introduce a global / thread-local for scope.** Scope flows through arguments, period. The whole point is to make implicit identity explicit.

## Known unknowns the prep phase should resolve

A. **What is the actual relationship between `app/services/turn_context.py` and `_resolve_scope`?** Is `TurnContext` already the abstraction we want, just incomplete? Or is it a separate concept (per-turn execution context: model handle, tool registry, audit log) distinct from per-event identity (`InboundScope`)? If the former, merge. If the latter, both exist and `TurnContext` takes an `InboundScope` as a field.

B. **What is the agentic turn entry point?** Probably `app/services/agentic.py`. Map its signature today and what it would look like with `scope` threaded through. Specifically: how do tool calls (`send_text`, etc.) currently get their `bot_id`? Closure-bound at turn construction, kwarg per call, or global? This is the single highest-risk area — if there's a hidden global, the refactor needs to remove it.

C. **What is the actual fourth leak the user is still seeing?** With patches (1) and (2) both on `main` and Rosi still misrouting, there must be at least one more code path the bot identity is being silently overwritten in. Trace a single inbound DM to Rosi from gateway to outbound and identify where bot_id flips to "mediator". Candidates: the agentic turn (B above), one of the three known hardcodes (recovery/vision/transcription), the OOB check (`oob_check.py`), or something not yet enumerated. **Find it before designing the refactor in detail; the location may inform the abstraction.**

D. **Coalescer shape decision**: after `f29bc03`, `app.state.coalescers` is a per-bot dict. Is per-bot the right model going forward, or should we collapse to one coalescer whose bursts carry scope and route by `burst.scope.bot_id`? Trade-off: per-bot isolation (current) is simpler but each bot needs its own loop overhead; single coalescer is leaner but the dispatch closure must read from burst, not lexical scope. Recommend the simpler-to-reason-about option; document the choice.

E. **`hot_context_solo.py` and `prompts_solo.py` already exist.** Confirm they are wired to be picked correctly when `scope.dyad_id is None`, or document what needs to change. Solo-shape must work for Rosi out of the box after this refactor — that's the whole point.

F. **The OOB delivery-time guardrail** (`app/services/oob_check.py`): how is it currently scoped? If it's global-to-recipient, it could suppress Rosi's reply because Véas just sent a message to the same user. Needs to be bot-aware. Prep should determine the current shape and the cleanest fix.

G. **Recovery path**: `recovery.py` runs at startup and replays missed messages. It has no live gateway event to scope from, so scope must be **reconstructed from DB state**. Prep should propose a helper signature like `scope_from_message_row(pool, row) -> InboundScope` and verify all needed fields are recoverable from the schema.

H. **`seed_weekly_reflections` working-tree-only import in `app/main.py:24`** — this rename is in the user's uncommitted working tree but not in any commit. Railway works because `railway up` uploads the working tree, but a fresh checkout (CI, worktree, anyone else) cannot import `app.main`. **The execute phase must commit this rename or otherwise reconcile** so the refactor PR is testable from a fresh checkout. Don't ignore this — it will block testing.

## Out of scope (do not touch in this refactor)

- Multi-WA-bot support (one WA bot for now; the abstraction will support multi-WA later but no new WA bots are being added).
- New persona authoring or prompt content changes (Rosi's persona is done).
- Discord intent / token / permission setup (already verified working).
- Schema changes (the `bot_id` columns and channel rows already exist).
- The `multi-agent-architecture.md` doc — that's tracked separately by a different sprint.

## How to verify (in addition to test suite)

The plan should describe a concrete manual verification ritual:

1. `pytest` passes (full suite).
2. `grep -rn 'bot_id="mediator"' app/` and `grep -rn 'bot_id: str = "mediator"' app/` return zero hits.
3. After deploy: send a Discord DM to Rosi (user ID `1401572203954311229`). Expected: German reply from Rosi's persona, pregnancy topic, within ~15s.
4. After deploy: send a Discord DM to Véas. Expected: English reply from Véas's persona, relationship topic, within ~15s.
5. Inspect the DB after step 3: the inbound `messages` row and the outbound `messages` row both have `bot_id='tante_rosi'` and the pregnancy `topic_id`.
6. Inspect the DB after step 4: both rows have `bot_id='mediator'` and the relationship `topic_id`.

        Approved plan:
        # Implementation Plan: InboundScope refactor - make per-event bot identity explicit

## Overview

**Goal.** Replace the loose `bot_id` kwarg/default pattern across the inbound-to-outbound path with a frozen `InboundScope` dataclass constructed at live gateway boundaries or reconstructed from durable job/recovery state, then passed through `process_inbound` -> coalescer -> agentic turn -> tools -> outbound. The refactor must stop Rosi traffic from being silently reinterpreted as mediator traffic.

**Root-cause check before flag fixes.** None of the remaining critique flags suggest the plan is targeting the wrong code or root cause. The production misroute is still downstream identity overwrite, especially `app/services/agentic.py::_run_agentic` selecting the bot from global settings instead of the event. The flags are plan-correctness issues around import topology, exact recovery column names, and preserving a second loose outbound identity API. These are direct adjustments to the same approach, not a reason to redesign it.

**Settled architecture decisions.**
- `InboundScope` lives in a new low-dependency module: `app/services/scope.py`. It does not live in `inbound.py`, because `inbound.py -> scheduled_job_handlers.py -> agentic.py` already forms an import chain that would circularly re-enter `inbound.py` once agentic imports the scope type.
- Keep both `InboundScope` and `TurnContext`: `InboundScope` is immutable per-event identity; `TurnContext` remains per-turn execution state.
- Keep `app.state.coalescers: dict[str, BurstCoalescer]`; per-bot gateway/client/pacer isolation stays.
- Production outbound uses `scope` as the identity carrier. `send_outbound` and `send_outbound_part` require `scope: InboundScope`; they do not expose a general fallback API accepting loose `bot_id`/`topic_id` pairs.
- Live gateway boundaries are authoritative for `transport` and provider channel metadata. Recovery and scheduled jobs may only construct scope from data that is actually present, or skip/fail clearly.
- WhatsApp remains mediator-only for now; the WA router is the documented boundary that explicitly constructs mediator scope.
- No DB schema changes, no prompt/persona changes, no globals/thread-locals for scope.
- `settings.bot_id` is renamed to `default_seed_bot_id` and used only by seed/test harness code that creates new scheduled rows without an inbound event. Agentic runtime never reads it.

## Phase 1: Pre-flight - unblock fresh checkouts

### Step 1: Commit the `seed_weekly_summaries` -> `seed_weekly_reflections` rename (`app/services/scheduled_job_handlers.py`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Verify** the HEAD mismatch: `git show HEAD:app/services/scheduled_job_handlers.py | grep '^def seed_weekly'` versus `git show HEAD:app/main.py | grep seed_weekly_reflections`.
2. **Inspect** the working-tree rename and companion imports in `app/main.py` and `app/services/inbound.py`.
3. **Run** `python -c 'import app.main'` before and after the isolated rename commit.
4. **Commit** only the rename/import reconciliation first so a fresh checkout can import `app.main` before the InboundScope refactor begins.

## Phase 2: Define scope without import cycles

### Step 2: Add canonical scope module (`app/services/scope.py`, `app/services/turn_context.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Create** `app/services/scope.py` as the canonical low-dependency home for `InboundScope`:
   ```python
   @dataclass(frozen=True)
   class InboundScope:
       """Immutable per-event identity; TurnContext remains per-turn execution state."""
       bot_id: str
       transport: Literal["discord", "whatsapp"] | None
       user_id: UUID
       topic_id: UUID | None
       channel_id: str | None
       binding_id: UUID | None
       dyad_id: UUID | None
   ```
2. **Import** `InboundScope` from `app.services.scope` everywhere: `inbound.py`, `debouncer.py`, `agentic.py`, `messaging.py`, `turn_context.py`, `recovery.py`, scheduled handlers, media handlers, tools, and tests.
3. **Do not** define or re-export the canonical type from `inbound.py`. A temporary `ResolvedScope = InboundScope` alias inside `inbound.py` is allowed only while migrating local call sites, and must be removed before merge if no callers remain.
4. **Extend** `obs_fields` to include `transport`, `user_id`, and `dyad_id` when present.
5. **Add** `TurnContext.from_scope(...)` to copy `scope.bot_id`, `scope.binding_id`, `scope.dyad_id`, `scope.channel_id`, `scope.transport`, and topic identity into the context while preserving existing execution fields.
6. **Allow** `partner=None` for solo bots.

### Step 3: Build live scope only after user resolution (`app/services/inbound.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Replace** current `ResolvedScope` usage with `InboundScope` imported from `app.services.scope`.
2. **Do not** construct `InboundScope` before the inbound `User` exists. Split current processing into:
   - user phase: resolve or create the `User` from gateway payload/provider identity;
   - identity phase: ensure any required existing `user_identities` row exists for the provider identity, if the repo has such a helper/table;
   - scope phase: build `InboundScope` from that `User`, gateway-known `bot_id`/`transport`/provider channel data, and binding/topic resolution.
3. **Update** `_resolve_scope` so it receives `user: User` or `user_id: UUID` rather than discovering the user id itself.
4. **Use** `get_bot_spec(scope.bot_id)` and bot-specific primary-topic resolution where scope construction needs a default topic.
5. **Add** a missing-identity inbound test proving a first-time sender gets a `User` and any required identity row before `InboundScope(user_id=...)` is constructed.

### Step 4: Define reconstruction helpers with real columns only (`app/services/scope.py`, `app/services/recovery.py`, `app/services/scheduled_job_handlers.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Do not** select or use nonexistent `messages.channel_id`, `scheduled_jobs.channel_id`, or `bot_bindings.transport`. Do not infer transport from `whatsapp_message_id`; Discord rows also populate that provider-message-id field.
2. **Add** `scope_from_message_row(pool, row) -> InboundScope | None`: read only available message fields such as `id`, `sender_id`, `bot_id`, and `topic_id`. Resolve `binding_id`/`dyad_id` using `routing.resolve_binding(pool, bot_id=row['bot_id'], user_id=row['sender_id'])`. Set `transport=None` and `channel_id=None` unless an existing durable table actually provides those values.
3. **Add** `scope_from_bot_turn_row(pool, turn_row) -> InboundScope | None`: crashed-turn replay must use the actual `bot_turns.user_in_context` column. Normalize at the query boundary with `user_in_context AS user_id`, or name `user_in_context` explicitly in the helper. It must not read a nonexistent `bot_turns.user_id` column.
4. **For crashed turns**, fetch the first triggering message only for topic consistency/fallback when needed; do not infer transport from that message's `whatsapp_message_id`.
5. **Add** `scope_from_job_row(pool, job_row) -> InboundScope | None`: read `bot_id`, `topic_id`, and `user_id` from `scheduled_jobs`, resolve binding/dyad if possible, and set `transport=None`/`channel_id=None` unless durable data exists.
6. **Fallback policy:** no helper may fabricate `transport='discord'`, `transport='whatsapp'`, `channel_id`, or mediator identity. If a downstream operation requires missing delivery context, skip or fail that replay/job with a clear log/heartbeat. Legacy recovery rows without enough durable context may be skipped.
7. **Tests** must cover that `whatsapp_message_id` is ignored for transport inference and that ambiguous legacy recovery/job rows are skipped or failed clearly instead of guessed.

## Phase 3: Thread scope through coalescer and agentic core

### Step 5: Thread scope through `BurstCoalescer` (`app/services/debouncer.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Replace** `_Burst.bot_id` with `_Burst.scope: InboundScope`; composite keys use `scope.bot_id`.
2. **Change** `on_burst_complete` to `Callable[[list[UUID], User, InboundScope], Awaitable[None]]`.
3. **Change** paced callbacks to accept scope: `on_paced_answer(message_ids, user, decision, scope)` and `on_paced_reaction(message_ids, user, decision, scope)`.
4. **Change** `add(...)` and `add_burst(...)` to require `scope`; remove loose `bot_id` from those APIs.
5. **Update** `_handle_ready_burst`, `_fire_after_wait`, `_fire_batch`, and `_call_paced_answer` so the unpaced path also calls `on_burst_complete(message_ids, user, burst.scope)`.

### Step 6: Make `_run_agentic` scope-driven (`app/services/agentic.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Change** `_run_agentic` to require keyword-only `scope: InboundScope`.
2. **Replace** `get_bot_spec(settings.bot_id)` with `get_bot_spec(scope.bot_id)`. This is the core root-cause fix.
3. **Derive** `primary_topic_id = scope.topic_id or await primary_topic_id_for(active_pool, bot_spec)` with a comment that scope wins and scheduled handlers must populate `scope.topic_id` for non-primary topics.
4. **Construct** `TurnContext` via `TurnContext.from_scope(...)`; remove identity fields populated from globals or hardcoded `None`.
5. **Record** `channel_id`, `binding_id`, and `bot_id` from scope in turn metadata/events.
6. **Remove** `ctx.bot_id or 'mediator'`; use `ctx.bot_id` unconditionally.
7. **Change** public entry points to require keyword-only `scope`: `run_agentic_turn`, `run_agentic_turn_with_metadata`, `run_agentic_turn_with_pool`, `run_agentic_job`, and `run_agentic_job_with_pool`.
8. **Verify** `agentic.py` imports `InboundScope` only from `app.services.scope`, never from `inbound.py`.

### Step 7: Update app startup/coalescer wiring (`app/main.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Change** `_run_paced_agentic_turn` to accept keyword-only `scope`; remove `bot_id: str = 'mediator'`.
2. **Rewrite** direct `on_burst_complete=run_agentic_turn` bindings at `main.py:206` and `main.py:222` as adapters that pass `scope=scope` keyword-only.
3. **Update** `_build_coalescer_for_bot` so dispatch closures use the burst-provided scope. Non-dispatch glue may still capture `bot_id` for selecting the correct Discord client/pacer.
4. **Keep** `app.state.coalescers` per bot.

## Phase 4: Plug remaining runtime leaks

### Step 8: Pass scope through inbound/media/pause/reaction paths (`app/services/inbound.py`, `app/services/transcription.py`, `app/services/vision.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Thread** the per-message `scope` from `process_inbound` into `_send_pause_confirmation`, `_handle_pause_command`, coalescer `add`, `handle_voice`, and `handle_image`.
2. **Replace** pause confirmation `bot_id='mediator'` / relationship-topic use with `scope.bot_id` / `scope.topic_id`.
3. **Update** `_control_recipients`: for `participants_shape='solo'`, recipients are `[scope.user_id]` and `partner_of` is not called; dyadic mediator behavior remains unchanged.
4. **Update** `handle_voice` and `handle_image` signatures to require `scope`; replace their hardcoded mediator literals with `scope.bot_id` and `scope.topic_id`.
5. **Update** `_handle_reaction` logging/feedback so `extra['bot_id']` is required bot identity, never `bot_id or 'mediator'`.

### Step 9: Make outbound/OOB scope-only in production (`app/services/messaging.py`, `app/services/hooks.py`, `app/services/tools/write_tools.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Change** `send_outbound` and `send_outbound_part` to require `scope: InboundScope`. Remove public `bot_id` and `topic_id` identity kwargs from the production API. `topic_id` is derived from `scope.topic_id`; if a caller needs a different topic, it must pass an explicit replaced scope or use a narrowly named internal helper.
2. **If** an internal low-level helper is needed for tests or non-inbound utilities, keep it private and name it to make the escape hatch obvious, for example `_send_outbound_resolved(..., *, bot_id: str, topic_id: UUID | None, ...)`. Production inbound-to-outbound paths must not call it directly.
3. **Remove all defensive logging fallbacks in `messaging.py`:** lines currently shaped like `bot_id or 'mediator'` at approximately `messaging.py:264`, `messaging.py:390`, and `messaging.py:426` become unconditional scope-derived values.
4. **Update** `_call_oob_hook` to accept and pass both `topic_id=scope.topic_id` and `bot_id=scope.bot_id` context to the hook. Preserve legacy hook compatibility with an explicit TypeError fallback chain: canonical with bot/topic/protected owners -> with topic/protected owners -> protected owners without topic -> minimal pool/content/recipient -> two-arg legacy.
5. **Update every direct `_call_oob_hook` caller, not only `send_outbound`:** `write_tools.py:506` (`send_bridge_candidate`) and `write_tools.py:1913` (`edit_outbound_message`) must pass `topic_id=ctx.primary_topic_id` and `bot_id=ctx.bot_id` explicitly.
6. **Update** `hooks._default_check_oob` to accept `topic_id: UUID | None = None` and pass it to `check_oob_with_policy(..., topic_id=topic_id)` instead of hardcoding `get_relationship_topic_id()`.
7. **Verify** `oob_check.check_oob_with_policy` already accepts optional `topic_id`; no policy rewrite is needed.

### Step 10: Reconstruct scope in recovery without fake transport (`app/services/recovery.py`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Expand** missed-message recovery SQL to select existing columns needed by `scope_from_message_row`: message `id`, `sender_id`, `bot_id`, and `topic_id`.
2. **Expand** crashed-turn recovery SQL/RETURNING to include the real bot-turn identity. Use `user_in_context AS user_id`, not `user_id`.
3. **Use** `scope_from_message_row` for missed-message replay; pass `scope=scope` into `coalescer.add` and preserve the pause-check comment.
4. **Use** `scope_from_bot_turn_row` for crashed-turn replay; pass `scope=scope` into `coalescer.add_burst`.
5. **If** a helper returns `None` because the row lacks real bot/user/topic identity or the available coalescer cannot handle that bot, skip/log the replay. Do not force non-mediator or unknown rows through mediator.
6. **Assert in tests** that a Discord message row with a non-null `whatsapp_message_id` is not reconstructed as WhatsApp scope. The old heuristic must be impossible to reintroduce unnoticed.

### Step 11: Fix scheduled-job scope and seed-time job creation (`app/services/scheduled_job_handlers.py`, `app/services/checkins.py`, `app/services/tools/write_tools.py`, `app/config.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Remove** `bot_id: str = 'mediator'` defaults from `write_tools._schedule_context_job` and `checkins.schedule_checkin_record`; callers must pass `ctx.bot_id`.
2. **For scheduled-job re-entry sites** at approximately lines `81`, `95`, `100`, `122`, and `127`, build scope with `scope_from_job_row` or `scope_from_message_row` when a triggering message exists, then pass `scope=scope` into `run_agentic_job` / `run_agentic_turn`.
3. **If** `scope_from_job_row` cannot build real bot/user/topic identity from the job row, mark/log the job according to existing failure semantics. Do not fabricate mediator or transport.
4. **Replace** relationship-topic fallbacks at approximately `scheduled_job_handlers.py:86` and `scheduled_job_handlers.py:163` with `primary_topic_id_for(pool, get_bot_spec(job['bot_id']))` when no explicit topic is present.
5. **Replace** `.get('bot_id', 'mediator')` fallbacks in scheduled-job handling, including `scheduled_job_handlers.py:110` heartbeat/logging extra and `scheduled_job_handlers.py:162` reschedule logic. Use `job['bot_id']` for existing rows; missing bot_id is a data error.
6. **Correct seed-time entry points instead of pretending a job row exists:** make `seed_weekly_reflection_for_user(..., *, bot_id: str, ...)` and `schedule_checkin_job(..., *, bot_id: str, ...)` require a keyword-only bot id. These functions create job rows, so they cannot read from a job row.
7. **Update mediator-only seed callers** such as the weekly-reflection seeding loop around line `272` to pass `bot_id=get_settings().default_seed_bot_id` or a bot-registry mediator constant, not `bot_id='mediator'`. Add a short comment that weekly reflections are mediator-only today.
8. **Rename** `Settings.bot_id` to `default_seed_bot_id` and preserve the existing `BOT_ID` env alias if required by the settings library/test harness. Runtime agentic code must not read this setting.

### Step 12: Strip tool-layer dispatch fallbacks (`app/services/tools/write_tools.py`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Remove operational Discord dispatch fallbacks** at `write_tools.py:1926`, `write_tools.py:1994`, and `write_tools.py:2053`: calls that currently pass `bot_id=ctx.bot_id or 'mediator'` must pass required `ctx.bot_id` only.
2. **Fail early if `ctx.bot_id` is missing** in these tool functions, matching the new invariant that `TurnContext` is always constructed from `InboundScope`.
3. **Audit nearby tool calls** with `rg "bot_id=.*or ['\"]mediator" app/services/tools` and remove any additional dispatch or logging fallback found.

### Step 13: Audit gateways and documented out-of-scope replay (`app/services/discord.py`, `app/routers/whatsapp.py`, `app/services/staging.py`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Discord**: `_handle_message`, `_handle_reaction_add`, and `catch_up_recent_messages` must supply the live boundary facts: `self.bot_id`, `transport='discord'`, provider user id, and provider channel id if available. `process_inbound` creates/upserts the user before constructing `InboundScope`.
2. **Discord reactions**: replace `get_relationship_topic_id()` in reaction handling with the bot-specific primary topic from `get_bot_spec(self.bot_id)` / `primary_topic_id_for`.
3. **WhatsApp**: keep single-bot behavior by explicitly supplying mediator bot identity and `transport='whatsapp'` at the router boundary. This is the only allowed `bot_id='mediator'` literal site in `app/routers/**`.
4. **Staging**: keep staging replay out of scope but add a TODO that explicitly names both gaps: hardcoded mediator/relationship-topic hot-context construction and `partner_of` assuming dyadic shape. File/follow a separate ticket for staging scope threading.

## Phase 5: Tests and verification wiring

### Step 14: Update fixtures and existing tests (`tests/conftest.py`, affected test files)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Add** `inbound_scope_mediator`, `inbound_scope_rosi`, and `inbound_scope_whatsapp` fixtures. Recovery/job fixtures may use `transport=None` only when they are explicitly testing non-live reconstructed scope.
2. **Update** `tests/test_send_outbound.py` by first running `grep -nE 'send_outbound\(' tests/test_send_outbound.py`; every call must pass `scope`. Tests should not switch to loose `bot_id`/`topic_id` kwargs because those are intentionally removed from the production API.
3. **Update** monkeypatched agentic fakes in `tests/test_main_startup_pacing.py`, `tests/test_scheduled_jobs.py`, and `tests/test_coach_e2e.py` to accept keyword-only `scope` and assert it is forwarded where meaningful.
4. **Update** `tests/test_pause_resume.py` and inbound/coalescer tests for the new scope API.
5. **Add** a solo-bot pause/control-recipient test proving `partner_of` is not called for `participants_shape='solo'`.
6. **Add** scheduled-job and recovery reconstruction tests covering ambiguous/missing transport: the job or replay is skipped/failed with a clear error when transport/channel is required, not stamped as Discord or WhatsApp based on `whatsapp_message_id`.
7. **Add** OOB caller coverage for direct `_call_oob_hook` uses in write tools: `send_bridge_candidate` and `edit_outbound_message` pass `ctx.primary_topic_id` and `ctx.bot_id` through.
8. **Add** tool dispatch coverage or assertions for the write-tools edit/delete/react calls so `ctx.bot_id` is required and no mediator fallback is used.
9. **Add** a missing-identity inbound test: a first-time sender gets a `User` and any required identity row before `InboundScope(user_id=...)` is constructed.
10. **Add** an import-topology test or smoke check that importing `app.services.agentic`, `app.services.inbound`, and `app.main` succeeds after `InboundScope` moves to `app/services/scope.py`.

### Step 15: Add Rosi routing regression test (`tests/test_inbound_scope_routing.py`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Drive** an inbound event for `bot_id='tante_rosi'` through `process_inbound`/coalescer/agentic with LLM side effects mocked.
2. **Assert** inbound row has `bot_id='tante_rosi'` and Rosi topic.
3. **Assert** constructed `TurnContext` uses `bot_id='tante_rosi'`, Rosi bot spec, and `participants_shape='solo'`.
4. **Assert** outbound row and OOB hook receive Rosi's bot/topic identity via `scope`.
5. **Confirm** this test fails on old HEAD because `_run_agentic` reads global mediator settings, and passes after the refactor.

### Step 16: Grep gates and full test run
**Scope:** Small
1. **Run** `grep -rnE "bot_id\s*=\s*['\"]mediator['\"]" app/services app/routers`; only the WhatsApp router boundary constructor is allowed. Prefer `default_seed_bot_id` or a registry constant for mediator-only seed code so scheduled handlers do not fail this grep.
2. **Run** `grep -rnE "bot_id\s*:\s*str\s*=\s*['\"]mediator['\"]" app/services app/routers`; expect zero hits.
3. **Run** `grep -rnE "bot_id\s*or\s*['\"]mediator['\"]" app/services app/routers`; expect zero hits.
4. **Run** `grep -rnE "\.get\(\s*['\"]bot_id['\"]\s*,\s*['\"]mediator['\"]" app/services app/routers`; expect zero hits.
5. **Run** `rg "send_outbound\(|send_outbound_part\(" app tests`; production call sites must pass `scope=` and tests should use scope fixtures, not loose identity kwargs.
6. **Run** `rg "_call_oob_hook" app/services`; every production caller must pass explicit bot/topic context, except deliberately documented legacy test hooks.
7. **Run** targeted tests first: coalescer, inbound source, main startup pacing, send outbound, scheduled jobs, pause/resume, write-tools OOB/dispatch, recovery reconstruction, import-topology, and the new routing regression.
8. **Run** full `pytest`; only the documented unrelated pre-existing failures may remain.

## Phase 6: Post-deploy verification

### Step 17: Manual runtime verification
**Scope:** Small
1. **Deploy** after merge.
2. **DM** Tante Rosi from Discord user `1401572203954311229`; expect German reply, Rosi persona, pregnancy topic, within about 15 seconds.
3. **DM** Veas; expect English reply, mediator persona, relationship topic, within about 15 seconds.
4. **Inspect** DB rows: Rosi inbound/outbound rows both have `bot_id='tante_rosi'` and Rosi topic; Veas rows both have `bot_id='mediator'` and relationship topic.
5. **Inspect** OOB logs and confirm the topic id matches the bot/topic for each outbound path.
6. **If** Rosi still misroutes after this refactor, investigate routing/channel seeding (`resolve_bot`, Rosi channel records, DEBT-044/050) as a separate follow-up. InboundScope guarantees faithful downstream identity only after the gateway has identified the correct bot.

## Execution Order

1. Land the rename/import pre-flight commit first.
2. Add `app/services/scope.py` and migrate imports before changing runtime signatures, preventing circular-import churn.
3. Reorder inbound user creation before `InboundScope` construction, then add reconstruction helpers and `TurnContext.from_scope`.
4. Thread coalescer and agentic core next; this closes the root cause.
5. Wire inbound/media/outbound/recovery/scheduled jobs after the core API is stable.
6. Enforce scope-only outbound and remove tool-layer dispatch/OOB fallbacks before relying on grep gates.
7. Update tests and run grep gates last, using the greps as authoritative cleanup checks.

## Validation Order

1. After Phase 1: `python -c 'import app.main'` succeeds.
2. After Phase 2: run import-topology, missing-identity inbound, and reconstruction tests to prove no circular import, no pre-user scope construction, and no fake transport.
3. After Phase 3: run coalescer, inbound-source, main-startup-pacing, and coach e2e targeted tests.
4. After Phase 4: run send-outbound, scheduled-jobs, pause/resume, media-path, recovery-reconstruction, write-tools dispatch, and OOB-caller targeted tests.
5. After Phase 5: run the grep gates, `_call_oob_hook` caller audit, scope-only outbound caller audit, `tests/test_inbound_scope_routing.py`, and full `pytest`.
6. After deploy: run the two Discord DM smoke tests, DB inspection, and OOB-log inspection.

    "`InboundScope` must live in `app/services/scope.py` to avoid circular imports. No runtime module should import it from `app.services.inbound`.",
    "Recovery and scheduled-job reconstruction must not infer transport from `whatsapp_message_id`; Discord rows also populate that provider-message-id field. Use `transport=None` and `channel_id=None` for reconstructed rows unless durable data truly exists.",
    "Crashed-turn recovery must use `bot_turns.user_in_context AS user_id` or explicitly handle `user_in_context`; there is no `bot_turns.user_id` column.",
    "Production outbound should require scope. Do not preserve a public loose `bot_id`/`topic_id` identity API that can recreate the leak.",
    "Update or remove `tests/test_messaging_non_agentic_bot_id.py`; it asserts the old loose bot_id contract and mediator hardcodes.",
    "The WhatsApp router is the only allowed explicit `bot_id='mediator'` boundary literal in `app/routers/**` or `app/services/**`. Seed code should use `default_seed_bot_id` or a registry constant.",
    "Do not fabricate mediator identity in logs, heartbeats, rescheduling, tool dispatch, recovery, or OOB checks. Grep gates should catch `or 'mediator'` and `.get('bot_id', 'mediator')`.",
    "Scheduled seed-time functions create job rows, so they cannot read a job row; make `bot_id` explicit keyword-only and pass a seed-only default from settings where mediator-only behavior is intentional.",
    "Keep per-bot Discord gateway/client/pacer/coalescer architecture. The coalescer may be per-bot, but burst dispatch must still read scope from the burst, not closure-captured identity.",
    "Do not change DB schemas or prompt/persona content. This is a wiring refactor only.",
    "Do not make staging replay worse; add the explicit TODO for hardcoded topic and dyadic `partner_of` gaps, but keep staging scope threading out of this refactor.",
    "Manual post-deploy verification may reveal a separate routing/channel seeding issue if the gateway itself identifies Rosi traffic as mediator; treat that as follow-up, not a reason to weaken downstream scope invariants.",
    "The four unrelated pre-existing test failures may remain, but new failures from this refactor must be fixed."

  "robustness": "standard",
  "signals": {
    "iteration": 7,
    "idea": "# InboundScope refactor \u2014 eliminate bot_id leaks across the inbound\u2192outbound path\n\n## Why this exists\n\nThe Veas codebase is being extended from a single-bot mediator (V\u00e9as) to a multi-bot platform. The second bot, Tante Rosi (German pregnancy coach, solo-shape, Discord-only), keeps misrouting: messages sent to Rosi get processed and replied by V\u00e9as, with the wrong persona, language, and topic.\n\nWe've now patched the *same shape of bug* three times in rapid succession:\n\n1. `app/services/inbound.py::_resolve_scope` had `bot_id: str = \"mediator\"` as a silent default \u2192 inbound rows tagged with the wrong bot. (Fixed in commit `5a38d7f`.)\n2. `app/main.py` constructed a single `app.state.coalescer` with `bot_id=\"mediator\"` captured in closures used as the dispatch callback. The burst data structure already carried the correct per-event bot_id, but the closure ignored it. (Fixed in commit `f29bc03` by moving to `app.state.coalescers: dict[str, BurstCoalescer]` and resolving topic via `get_bot_spec(bot_id) + primary_topic_id_for(pool, spec)`.)\n3. `app/services/recovery.py`, `app/services/vision.py`, `app/services/transcription.py` still hardcode `bot_id='mediator'` in their own outbound calls. (Not yet fixed.)\n\nAfter both patches (1) and (2) were on `main`, the user reports the wrong bot is **still responding**. So there is at least one more leak we haven't characterised yet, plus the three known hardcodes above.\n\n**This is no longer a patching problem \u2014 it's a structural one.** Each fix surfaces another instance of the same shape because `bot_id` was bolted onto a codebase that was written assuming a single mediator. It rides through the system as a loose parameter: sometimes a function arg, sometimes a closure capture, sometimes a DB column, sometimes a default. The boundary between \"this code knows its bot\" and \"this code looks it up\" is fuzzy and inconsistent.\n\n## What we want\n\nIntroduce a single explicit **`InboundScope`** dataclass (frozen) that:\n\n- Is constructed **once** at each gateway boundary (Discord `_handle_message`, Discord `_handle_reaction_add`, Discord `catch_up_recent_messages`, WhatsApp router).\n- Carries **all per-event identity**: `bot_id`, `transport`, `user_id` (sender), `topic_id`, `channel_id`, `binding_id`, `dyad_id` (None for solo-shape bots like Rosi).\n- Threads explicitly through every downstream code path: `process_inbound` \u2192 coalescer \u2192 burst dispatch \u2192 agentic turn \u2192 LLM tools \u2192 outbound (text, voice, image) \u2192 DB writes.\n- **Replaces** the existing loose `bot_id` kwarg pattern. No function below the gateway accepts `bot_id` as an optional kwarg with a `\"mediator\"` default. Forgetting to pass scope becomes a type error.\n\nThe existing `ResolvedScope` NamedTuple in `app/services/inbound.py` (around line 161) is the seed of this \u2014 it already has `(bot_id, topic_id, channel_id, binding_id, dyad_id)`. **Promote it to a frozen dataclass, add `transport` and `user_id`, rename to `InboundScope`, and make it the universal carrier.**\n\nThere is also an existing `app/services/turn_context.py` \u2014 investigate during prep whether it already plays a related role and whether it should be merged with / replaced by `InboundScope`, or whether they have distinct responsibilities (per-event identity vs per-turn execution context).\n\n## Done looks like\n\n1. **Defined**: `InboundScope` dataclass exists in one canonical location (probably `app/services/inbound.py` or a new `app/services/scope.py`). Frozen, fully typed, no defaults.\n2. **Threaded**: every function from gateway \u2192 outbound takes scope (or scope-derived values) explicitly. Audited list of touched files includes at minimum:\n   - `app/services/discord.py` (gateway handlers: `_handle_message`, `_handle_reaction_add`, `catch_up_recent_messages`)\n   - `app/routers/whatsapp.py` (WA webhook \u2192 constructs `InboundScope(bot_id=\"mediator\", transport=\"whatsapp\", ...)` explicitly \u2014 single-WA-bot is fine for now)\n   - `app/services/inbound.py` (`process_inbound`, `_resolve_scope`)\n   - `app/services/debouncer.py` (coalescer: `add(scope, ...)`, burst carries scope, on_complete callback uses `burst.scope` not closure)\n   - `app/services/agentic.py` and/or `app/services/turn_context.py` (agentic turn takes scope)\n   - `app/services/tools/*` (LLM tools: `send_text`, distillation tools, etc. \u2014 take scope or scope-derived bot_id/topic_id, no defaults)\n   - `app/services/messaging.py` (outbound send: takes scope; the OOB delivery-time guardrail also needs to be bot-aware \u2014 see open question below)\n   - `app/services/transcription.py` (voice path: takes scope; remove `bot_id='mediator'` hardcode at lines ~77, 94, 118)\n   - `app/services/vision.py` (image path: takes scope; remove `bot_id='mediator'` hardcode at lines ~198, 218, 225)\n   - `app/services/recovery.py` (startup replay: reconstructs scope from DB row via a helper `scope_from_message_row(row)`; remove `bot_id='mediator'` hardcode at line ~114)\n   - `app/services/hot_context.py` and `app/services/hot_context_solo.py` (audit: confirm they take topic_id/scope cleanly; solo variant must work with `dyad_id=None`)\n   - `app/services/prompts.py` and `app/services/prompts_solo.py` (audit: confirm renderer pick is by scope.dyad_id is None or by bot spec; no hidden \"always mediator\" assumption)\n   - `app/services/oob_check.py` (audit: the delivery-time OOB guardrail must be scoped per bot, not global to recipient)\n3. **Defaults removed**: zero occurrences of `bot_id: str = \"mediator\"` or `bot_id=\"mediator\"` in `app/services/**` and `app/routers/**` after the refactor. Grep verification is part of the success criteria.\n4. **Closure leaks removed**: the coalescer's `on_burst_complete` callback reads `burst.scope` (or `burst.bot_id`) \u2014 does **not** capture bot_id from the surrounding closure. The per-bot dict introduced in `f29bc03` may stay or may collapse to one shared coalescer; design call during prep/plan.\n5. **Tests updated**: existing test suite passes (`pytest` from repo root). Tests that construct fake calls into `process_inbound`, `send_text`, etc. pass a proper `InboundScope` fixture rather than ad-hoc kwargs.\n6. **Behavior verified**: after the refactor, a DM to Rosi (Discord user ID `1401572203954311229`) gets a German reply from Rosi's persona with the pregnancy topic; a DM to V\u00e9as gets an English reply from V\u00e9as's persona with the relationship topic. Both bots online simultaneously, no cross-leaks. (Manual verification by user post-deploy; the plan should describe how to test it.)\n\n## Constraints / invariants to preserve\n\n- **Don't break V\u00e9as.** V\u00e9as is in production and being used. The refactor must preserve current dyadic-mediator behavior end-to-end.\n- **Don't break WhatsApp.** WA is currently mediator-only; the WA router must continue to work with `InboundScope(bot_id=\"mediator\", transport=\"whatsapp\", ...)`.\n- **Don't change DB schemas** in this refactor. The `bot_id` column already exists on messages, channels, etc. The refactor is code-only.\n- **Don't change the LLM prompts** for either bot (other than threading the right one for the right turn). Persona templates already exist in `app/services/prompts.py` and `app/services/prompts_solo.py`.\n- **Don't break the per-bot Discord gateway architecture** introduced earlier. Each bot has its own `DiscordClient`, `DiscordGatewayBot`, and `DiscordPacer`. These are correctly per-bot already \u2014 the leak is downstream of the gateway, not at the gateway.\n- **Don't introduce a global / thread-local for scope.** Scope flows through arguments, period. The whole point is to make implicit identity explicit.\n\n## Known unknowns the prep phase should resolve\n\nA. **What is the actual relationship between `app/services/turn_context.py` and `_resolve_scope`?** Is `TurnContext` already the abstraction we want, just incomplete? Or is it a separate concept (per-turn execution context: model handle, tool registry, audit log) distinct from per-event identity (`InboundScope`)? If the former, merge. If the latter, both exist and `TurnContext` takes an `InboundScope` as a field.\n\nB. **What is the agentic turn entry point?** Probably `app/services/agentic.py`. Map its signature today and what it would look like with `scope` threaded through. Specifically: how do tool calls (`send_text`, etc.) currently get their `bot_id`? Closure-bound at turn construction, kwarg per call, or global? This is the single highest-risk area \u2014 if there's a hidden global, the refactor needs to remove it.\n\nC. **What is the actual fourth leak the user is still seeing?** With patches (1) and (2) both on `main` and Rosi still misrouting, there must be at least one more code path the bot identity is being silently overwritten in. Trace a single inbound DM to Rosi from gateway to outbound and identify where bot_id flips to \"mediator\". Candidates: the agentic turn (B above), one of the three known hardcodes (recovery/vision/transcription), the OOB check (`oob_check.py`), or something not yet enumerated. **Find it before designing the refactor in detail; the location may inform the abstraction.**\n\nD. **Coalescer shape decision**: after `f29bc03`, `app.state.coalescers` is a per-bot dict. Is per-bot the right model going forward, or should we collapse to one coalescer whose bursts carry scope and route by `burst.scope.bot_id`? Trade-off: per-bot isolation (current) is simpler but each bot needs its own loop overhead; single coalescer is leaner but the dispatch closure must read from burst, not lexical scope. Recommend the simpler-to-reason-about option; document the choice.\n\nE. **`hot_context_solo.py` and `prompts_solo.py` already exist.** Confirm they are wired to be picked correctly when `scope.dyad_id is None`, or document what needs to change. Solo-shape must work for Rosi out of the box after this refactor \u2014 that's the whole point.\n\nF. **The OOB delivery-time guardrail** (`app/services/oob_check.py`): how is it currently scoped? If it's global-to-recipient, it could suppress Rosi's reply because V\u00e9as just sent a message to the same user. Needs to be bot-aware. Prep should determine the current shape and the cleanest fix.\n\nG. **Recovery path**: `recovery.py` runs at startup and replays missed messages. It has no live gateway event to scope from, so scope must be **reconstructed from DB state**. Prep should propose a helper signature like `scope_from_message_row(pool, row) -> InboundScope` and verify all needed fields are recoverable from the schema.\n\nH. **`seed_weekly_reflections` working-tree-only import in `app/main.py:24`** \u2014 this rename is in the user's uncommitted working tree but not in any commit. Railway works because `railway up` uploads the working tree, but a fresh checkout (CI, worktree, anyone else) cannot import `app.main`. **The execute phase must commit this rename or otherwise reconcile** so the refactor PR is testable from a fresh checkout. Don't ignore this \u2014 it will block testing.\n\n## Out of scope (do not touch in this refactor)\n\n- Multi-WA-bot support (one WA bot for now; the abstraction will support multi-WA later but no new WA bots are being added).\n- New persona authoring or prompt content changes (Rosi's persona is done).\n- Discord intent / token / permission setup (already verified working).\n- Schema changes (the `bot_id` columns and channel rows already exist).\n- The `multi-agent-architecture.md` doc \u2014 that's tracked separately by a different sprint.\n\n## How to verify (in addition to test suite)\n\nThe plan should describe a concrete manual verification ritual:\n\n1. `pytest` passes (full suite).\n2. `grep -rn 'bot_id=\"mediator\"' app/` and `grep -rn 'bot_id: str = \"mediator\"' app/` return zero hits.\n3. After deploy: send a Discord DM to Rosi (user ID `1401572203954311229`). Expected: German reply from Rosi's persona, pregnancy topic, within ~15s.\n4. After deploy: send a Discord DM to V\u00e9as. Expected: English reply from V\u00e9as's persona, relationship topic, within ~15s.\n5. Inspect the DB after step 3: the inbound `messages` row and the outbound `messages` row both have `bot_id='tante_rosi'` and the pregnancy `topic_id`.\n6. Inspect the DB after step 4: both rows have `bot_id='mediator'` and the relationship `topic_id`.\n\n## Repo / tooling notes\n\n- Project dir: `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/Veas`\n- Python: 3.11.11 via pyenv\n- Tests: `pytest` from repo root; full suite is currently ~779 tests with 4 pre-existing failures unrelated to this refactor \u2014 those 4 are acceptable to leave alone.\n- Deploy: Railway (uploads working tree). Service name: `Veas`. The user will run `railway up --ci --detach` after merge.\n- Branch: currently on `rosi-pregnancy-bot`. Refactor can land on `main` directly or on this branch \u2014 propose during plan.\n- Co-author trailer for commits (per repo convention): `Co-Authored-By: Claude Opus 4.7 <noreply@anthropic.com>`",

        Settled decisions (verify the executor implemented these correctly):
- scope-module: `InboundScope` lives in `app/services/scope.py`, not `inbound.py`. (This avoids the known inbound -> scheduled handlers -> agentic circular import path.)
- turn-context-separation: Keep `InboundScope` and `TurnContext` distinct. (`InboundScope` is immutable per-event identity; `TurnContext` remains per-turn execution state and should be built from scope.)
- outbound-contract: Production outbound APIs require `scope`; loose `bot_id`/`topic_id` identity kwargs are not preserved as a public path. (Keeping both APIs would retain the exact class of identity leak this refactor is meant to remove.)
- reconstructed-scope: Recovery and scheduled jobs may use `transport=None`/`channel_id=None` rather than fabricating delivery context. (The current schema does not durably store unambiguous transport/channel facts for all replay rows, and no schema change is in scope.)

    "python - <<'PY'\nimport ast\nfrom pathlib import Path\n\nroots = [Path('app/services'), Path('app/routers')]\nproblems = []\nfor root in roots:\n    for path in root.rglob('*.py'):\n        tree = ast.parse(path.read_text(), filename=str(path))\n        for node in ast.walk(tree):\n            if isinstance(node, ast.Call):\n                func = node.func\n                name = func.id if isinstance(func, ast.Name) else func.attr if isinstance(func, ast.Attribute) else None\n                if name in {'send_outbound', 'send_outbound_part'}:\n                    kw_names = {kw.arg for kw in node.keywords if kw.arg}\n                    if 'scope' not in kw_names:\n                        problems.append((path, node.lineno, name, 'missing scope='))\n                    forbidden = {'bot_id', 'topic_id'} & kw_names\n                    if forbidden:\n                        problems.append((path, node.lineno, name, f'loose identity kwargs: {sorted(forbidden)}'))\nfor p in problems:\n    print(f'{p[0]}:{p[1]}: {p[2]} {p[3]}')\nraise SystemExit(1 if problems else 0)\nPY",
    "python - <<'PY'\nimport ast\nfrom pathlib import Path\n\nroots = [Path('app/services'), Path('app/routers')]\nproblems = []\nfor root in roots:\n    for path in root.rglob('*.py'):\n        tree = ast.parse(path.read_text(), filename=str(path))\n        for node in ast.walk(tree):\n            if not isinstance(node, ast.Call):\n                continue\n            func = node.func\n            name = func.id if isinstance(func, ast.Name) else func.attr if isinstance(func, ast.Attribute) else None\n            if name != '_call_oob_hook':\n                continue\n            kw_names = {kw.arg for kw in node.keywords if kw.arg}\n            missing = {'bot_id', 'topic_id'} - kw_names\n            if missing:\n                problems.append((path, node.lineno, f'missing explicit context: {sorted(missing)}'))\nfor p in problems:\n    print(f'{p[0]}:{p[1]}: _call_oob_hook {p[2]}')\nraise SystemExit(1 if problems else 0)\nPY",

        Requirements:
        - Verify each success criterion explicitly.
        - Trust executor evidence by default. Dig deeper only where the git diff, `execution_audit.json`, or vague notes make the claim ambiguous.
        - Each criterion has a `priority` (`must`, `should`, or `info`). Apply these rules:
          - `must` criteria are hard gates. A `must` criterion that fails means `needs_rework`.
          - `should` criteria are quality targets. If the spirit is met but the letter is not, mark `pass` with evidence explaining the gap. Only mark `fail` if the intent was clearly missed. A `should` failure alone does NOT require `needs_rework`.
          - `info` criteria are for human reference. Mark them `waived` with a note — do not evaluate them.
          - If a criterion has `requires` capabilities that are not satisfiable by container workers (e.g., `drive_browser`, `subjective_judgment`), mark it `deferred_human` — NOT `fail` or `waived`. Deferred-human criteria do NOT count toward `needs_rework`.
          - If a criterion (any priority) cannot be verified in this context (e.g., requires manual testing or runtime observation), mark it `waived` with an explanation.
        - Set `review_verdict` to `needs_rework` only when at least one `must` criterion fails or actual implementation work is incomplete. Use `approved` when all `must` criteria pass, even if some `should` criteria are flagged.
        - The decisions listed above were settled at the gate stage. Verify that the executor implemented each settled decision correctly. Flag deviations from these decisions, but do not question the decisions themselves.
        - baseline_test_failures in finalize.json lists tests that were already failing before execution. Do not flag these as rework items unless the executor introduced new failures in those same tests.
        - Cross-reference each task's `files_changed` and `commands_run` against the git diff and any audit findings.
        - Review every `sense_check` explicitly and treat perfunctory acknowledgments as a reason to dig deeper.
        - Follow this JSON shape exactly:
        ```json
        {
          "review_verdict": "approved",
          "criteria": [
            {
              "name": "All existing tests pass",
              "priority": "must",
              "pass": "pass",
              "evidence": "Test suite ran green — 42 passed, 0 failed."
            },
            {
              "name": "File under ~300 lines",
              "priority": "should",
              "pass": "pass",
              "evidence": "File is 375 lines — above the target but reasonable given the component's responsibilities. Spirit met."
            },
            {
              "name": "Manual smoke tests pass",
              "priority": "info",
              "pass": "waived",
              "evidence": "Cannot be verified in automated review. Noted for manual QA."
            }
          ],
          "issues": [],
          "rework_items": [],
          "summary": "Approved. All must criteria pass. The should criterion on line count is close enough given the component scope.",
          "task_verdicts": [
            {
              "task_id": "T6",
              "reviewer_verdict": "Pass. Claimed handler changes and command evidence match the repo state.",
              "evidence_files": ["megaplan/handlers.py", "megaplan/evaluation.py"]
            }
          ],
          "sense_check_verdicts": [
            {
              "sense_check_id": "SC6",
              "verdict": "Confirmed. The execute blocker only fires when both evidence arrays are empty."
            }
          ]
        }
        ```
        - `rework_items` must be an array of structured rework directives. When `review_verdict` is `needs_rework`, populate one entry per issue with:
          - `task_id`: which finalize task this issue relates to
          - `issue`: what is wrong
          - `expected`: what correct behavior looks like
          - `actual`: what was observed
          - `evidence_file` (optional): file path supporting the finding
          - `flag_id`: critique/review flag ID when applicable, otherwise `null`
          - `source`: short machine-readable source tag when applicable, otherwise `null`
        - `issues` must still be populated as a flat one-line-per-item summary derived from `rework_items` (for backward compatibility). When approved, both `issues` and `rework_items` should be empty arrays.
        - When the work needs another execute pass, keep the same shape and change only `review_verdict` to `needs_rework`; make `issues`, `rework_items`, `summary`, and task verdicts specific enough for the executor to act on directly.
